Write tile and CGB attributes at the LCDC window map; DMG receives only the tile write. Use the access-checked write path.
__settilewincgb_unsafe

Write a tile number and its CGB attribute. On DMG, only the tile-number write is performed. On CGB, enter with VRAM bank 0 selected; attributes use bank 1 and the call finishes with VBK=0.

LCDC bit 6 selects the window map: zero uses 0x9800, one uses 0x9C00. These are map coordinates, not the window's screen position.

Does not wait for VRAM access. Call only while the LCD is off or within a caller-managed writable interval with enough time for the whole transfer.

Syntax

c
void __settilewincgb_unsafe(u8 x, u8 y, u8 tile, u8 attr);

Parameters

x, y

Coordinates in 8×8 tiles, each from 0 to 31. Keep the complete write inside the 32×32 map; coordinates are not clipped.

tile

Tile number, 0–255. LCDC bit 4 determines how the number addresses tile-image data. It is not a VRAM byte address.

attr

CGB attribute byte: bits 0–2 select palette 0–7; bit 3 selects tile-data bank; bits 5 and 6 flip horizontally and vertically; bit 7 sets BG/window priority. Keep unused bit 4 zero.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

Usage example

c
// __settilewincgb_unsafe
// Write a tile number and its CGB attribute. On DMG, only the tile-number write is
// performed. On CGB, enter with VRAM bank 0 selected; attributes use bank 1 and the call
// finishes with `VBK=0`. LCDC bit 6 selects the window map: zero uses `0x9800`, one uses
// `0x9C00`. These are map coordinates, not the window's screen position.
// Expected: FAILED CHECKS 000, with the written tile data in the selected map.
#include "gb_tile_example.h"

void main() {
    u8 failures;
    failures = 0;
    tile_example_begin();
    __settilewincgb_unsafe(2, 3, 'A', KQ_CGB_ATTR(2, 0, 0, 0, 0));
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C62, 0) != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9862, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    if (__cgb_is_cgb() && tile_example_read(0x9C62, 1) != 2) { failures++; }
    if (__cgb_is_cgb() && tile_example_read(0x9862, 1) != 0) { failures++; }
    tile_example_finish(failures, 1);
}

Expected result

The selected map shows the written characters or filled area. The program reads back the addressed tile bytes and, on CGB, the attribute bytes; FAILED CHECKS 000 means those comparisons passed. Both maps initially contain zero, and the example selects 0x9C00 for BG/window before the demonstrated call, so a fixed-map call can be distinguished from a selected-map call. These examples perform the writes with the LCD off; they do not measure LCD-on timing.

On CGB the A is blue: attribute value 2 selects the blue palette prepared by this example. On DMG the same character is black because CGB attributes are not applied.

Expected image: the following tile pattern starts at tile coordinates (x=2, y=3), or pixel coordinates (16,24). Each character occupies one 8×8 tile. Read the pattern from top to bottom and left to right; the surrounding tiles remain blank.

text
A

Write tile and CGB attributes at the LCDC window map; DMG receives only the tile write. No VRAM access waits are inserted.

input.h — Button state, press, release and repeat

Use this library to separate continuous movement (input_down), one-shot actions (input_pressed), stopping on release (input_released) and menu repetition (input_repeat). Initialize once, update once per input tick, then query as often as needed. btn_down, btn_pressed, btn_released and btn_repeat are aliases for those four queries.

Shared masks: BTN_RIGHT=0x01, BTN_LEFT=0x02, BTN_UP=0x04, BTN_DOWN=0x08, BTN_A=0x10, BTN_B=0x20, BTN_SELECT=0x40, BTN_START=0x80. A set bit means held. This layout applies to input_* on both GB and FC; raw FC pad functions use a different order.

Repeat timing counts updates, not milliseconds. A new press emits immediately. With defaults INPUT_REPEAT_DELAY=18 and INPUT_REPEAT_RATE=5, the next pulse is 19 updates later, followed by pulses every 6 updates. Each button has its own counter. Define these macros consistently when compiling input.c; changing only the caller's header does not reconfigure the library.

__readpadex

Read all buttons and compare them with the supplied previous held mask. Pack the current mask into the low byte and newly pressed bits, current & ~previous, into the high byte. No previous state is stored internally and no release or repeat mask is returned.

Syntax

c
u16 __readpadex(u8 prev_keys);

Parameters

prev_keys

Previous full held mask from __readpad() or the low byte of the preceding __readpadex() result. Pass 0 on the first sample, then save the new low byte for the next call.

Return value

A u16: (u8)result is the current mask; (u8)(result >> 8) is the new-press mask. Store the result in a 16-bit variable or the press bits will be lost.

Conditions and limits

Shared masks: BTN_RIGHT=0x01, BTN_LEFT=0x02, BTN_UP=0x04, BTN_DOWN=0x08, BTN_A=0x10, BTN_B=0x20, BTN_SELECT=0x40, BTN_START=0x80. A set bit means held. This layout applies to input_* on both GB and FC; raw FC pad functions use a different order.

Usage example

c
packed_first=__readpadex(0); // First sample: current 0x11, new presses 0x11.
current_keys=(u8)packed_first;
press_keys=(u8)(packed_first>>8);
packed_held=__readpadex(current_keys); // Held without a change: new presses are 0.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

Use this program to compare the three button-mask layouts and the packed press result. Hold A and Right together. ALL KEYS is 17; DIRECTIONS and BUTTONS LOW are each 1. With a previous mask of 0, EX CURRENT and EX NEW PRESS are both 17. Passing the current mask to the next read makes HELD PRESS 0. FAILED CHECKS must be 0. The values remain on screen after the snapshot, so releasing the buttons does not erase them.

Complete example program

Build command

powershell
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force .\out | Out-Null
.\kitaqgb\kitaqgb.exe .\kitaq-docs\samples\api-examples\gb\input_raw.c -I .\kitaqgb\lib -I .\kitaq-docs\samples -o .\out\input_raw.gb --profile=dev --rst-disable --stack-bank=fixed --cgb=cgb --no-disasm --no-cache

__cgb_safe_set_bgpd

Write one byte to the background/window palette data register BCPD/BGPD at $FF69 on CGB. __cgb_safe_set_bcpd is another name for the same register. The associated index register selects the byte position; a full RGB15 color requires a low byte and a high byte.

Syntax

c
__cgb_safe_set_bgpd(/* 書式は実装と呼び出し例を参照 */);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

One byte, 0–255. Write the low byte followed by the high byte of RGB15; enable index auto-increment first, or explicitly select each position. A data write does not enable auto-increment itself.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Palette and guarded register writes do nothing in DMG mode. Here, safe means a CGB-mode guard, not a PPU access wait or a critical section. Use LCD-off setup or a sufficiently short update in an accessible PPU phase. These functions do not wait for VBlank or protect a multi-byte update from interrupts.

Usage example

c
__cgb_safe_set_bgpd(31); __cgb_safe_set_bgpd(0); // Alias of BCPD: red at slot 31.
expect_color(0,31,31);

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This program teaches how palette contents affect the same tile pattern differently in BG and OBJ. Each triangle is 8 × 8 pixels; its top row has one pixel on the left, expanding to a full bottom row. At x = 16, 32, 48, 64, 80, 96, 112, the BG row at y = 24 is red, blue, green, red, blue, green, red. The OBJ row at y = 64 is blue, green, red, blue, green, red, blue. The columns use single-color, component, indexed-range, full-palette, absolute-range, canonical-register and alias-register writes, respectively.

At y = 88, the seven 8 × 8 cells show: red triangle (x = 16), blue one-pixel square outline from bank 1 (32), horizontally reflected green triangle (48), vertically reflected red triangle (64), blue triangle reflected both ways (80), green triangle over a red sprite with red visible through color-zero pixels (96), and a full red sprite in front of the BG triangle as a priority control (112). Coordinates are screen pixels measured from the upper-left corner. Blank space and black text remain on white.

FAILED CHECKS 000 confirms RGB masking, truth-valued flags (including input 2), palette index wrapping, writes crossing a palette boundary, clamping at slot 31, null/zero-length no-ops and palette readback while LCD is off. Four target/hardware combinations are tested: dual-mode ROM on DMG and CGB, DMG-only on DMG, and CGB-only on CGB. On DMG, CGB MODE is 0, all triangles are black, the bank/flip attributes have no display effect, and the last two bottom cells are black squares. CGB MODE is 1 in CGB tests. Real hardware and LCD-on palette contention are not covered.

Complete example program

__cgb_safe_set_bgpi

Write the background/window palette byte-index register BCPS/BGPI at $FF68 on CGB. __cgb_safe_set_bcps writes the same register. This selects where a subsequent data-register access goes; it does not itself write a color.

Syntax

c
__cgb_safe_set_bgpi(/* 書式は実装と呼び出し例を参照 */);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

Byte index in bits 0–5 (0–63), with bit 7 set for auto-increment after data writes; bit 6 is unused. Color slot n starts at byte 2n. Auto-increment wraps after byte 63, so raw register writes require your own range control.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Palette and guarded register writes do nothing in DMG mode. Here, safe means a CGB-mode guard, not a PPU access wait or a critical section. Use LCD-off setup or a sufficiently short update in an accessible PPU phase. These functions do not wait for VBlank or protect a multi-byte update from interrupts.

c

At x = 16, 32, 48, 64, 80, 96, 112, the BG row at y = 24 is red, blue, green, red, blue, green, red. The OBJ row at y = 64 is blue, green, red, blue, green, red, blue. The columns use single-color, component, indexed-range, full-palette, absolute-range, canonical-register and alias-register writes, respectively.

At y = 88, the seven 8 × 8 cells show: red triangle (x = 16), blue one-pixel square outline from bank 1 (32), horizontally reflected green triangle (48), vertically reflected red triangle (64), blue triangle reflected both ways (80), green triangle over a red sprite with red visible through color-zero pixels (96), and a full red sprite in front of the BG triangle as a priority control (112). Coordinates are screen pixels measured from the upper-left corner. Blank space and black text remain on white.

Four target/hardware combinations are tested: dual-mode ROM on DMG and CGB, DMG-only on DMG, and CGB-only on CGB. __cgb_safe_set_hdma1

Write HDMA1 ($FF51), the high byte of the CGB DMA source address. This configures the source; it neither copies data nor starts a transfer. Set HDMA2 for the low byte.

Syntax

c
void __cgb_safe_set_hdma1(u8 value);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

Register value as u8 (0–255). The bit meanings for this particular register are given above; the function accepts a byte, not a pointer or a byte count.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Writes are guarded by the compiler's CGB-mode check and do nothing in DMG mode. Safe denotes this mode guard only. The call does not save or restore the previous register value.

One block is 16 bytes. New transfers support 1–128 blocks (16–2048 bytes), so control value 0 means 16 bytes, not an empty transfer. GDMA pauses the CPU until complete. HBlank DMA transfers one block per visible-line HBlank and runs across frames; the caller must poll or otherwise track completion before reusing its data.

Use GDMA with the LCD off or within a sufficiently long writable period. Start HBlank DMA with the LCD running, outside STAT mode 0; this sample starts it in VBlank. Do not change source banks, destination VBK or the source data while it is active. HALT pauses HBlank DMA too. No wait, interrupt protection or source/destination range check is added by these setters.

Read $FF55 separately: bit 7 clear means active; $FF means completed. After cancellation, bit 7 is set and bits 0–6 retain remaining blocks minus one. Cancellation does not undo copied bytes. Only issue a cancel command while a transfer is active; otherwise bit 7 clear starts GDMA. Keep the entire transfer inside valid source memory and the destination VRAM bank; do not rely on address overflow.

These HDMA register setters control CGB hardware DMA. __vram_copy_dma is a separate compiler alias for a CPU copy loop; its name does not make it use this DMA controller.

Usage example

c
__cgb_safe_set_hdma1(0xC6); // Source high byte: fixed-WRAM buffer at $C600.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

The complete program compares three transfer paths using a 16-byte-aligned buffer at $C600. GDMA copies 32 bytes to $8010: at screen (16,24) a red 8 × 8 triangle grows from one leftmost pixel to a full bottom row, followed at (24,24) by a red one-pixel square outline. HBlank DMA copies 32 bytes to $8030: at (16,48) a blue 4 × 8 left half-tile is followed at (24,48) by a blue 8 × 8 square. These are actual transferred tile patterns, not numerical placeholders.

The CANCELED row contains a green 4 × 8 half-tile at (16,72) and a green 8 × 8 square at (24,72), initially written by the CPU. A two-block HBlank transfer targeting these tiles is armed and canceled in the same VBlank, before its first block; $FF55 must read $81, and both patterns must remain unchanged after a frame. On DMG, the first two rows stay blank and only these two sentinels appear in black. Text remains black on white. FAILED CHECKS 000 also checks copied bytes and completion status. The test covers dual-mode DMG/CGB and CGB-only execution in KOKURA; partial cancellation, double speed and real hardware are not tested.

Complete example program

kitaqgb/kitaqgb/CodeGenerator.cs:1807

__cgb_safe_set_hdma2

Write HDMA2 ($FF52), the low byte of the DMA source address. Hardware ignores bits 0–3, aligning the source downward to 16 bytes. For example, HDMA1=$C6, HDMA2=$0F selects $C600. It does not start DMA.

GDMA copies 32 bytes to $8010: at screen (16,24) a red 8 × 8 triangle grows from one leftmost pixel to a full bottom row, followed at (24,24) by a red one-pixel square outline. HBlank DMA copies 32 bytes to $8030: at (16,48) a blue 4 × 8 left half-tile is followed at (24,48) by a blue 8 × 8 square. These are actual transferred tile patterns, not numerical placeholders.

The CANCELED row contains a green 4 × 8 half-tile at (16,72) and a green 8 × 8 square at (24,72), initially written by the CPU. A two-block HBlank transfer targeting these tiles is armed and canceled in the same VBlank, before its first block; $FF55 must read $81, and both patterns must remain unchanged after a frame. On DMG, the first two rows stay blank and only these two sentinels appear in black. Text remains black on white. FAILED CHECKS 000 also checks copied bytes and completion status. The test covers dual-mode DMG/CGB and CGB-only execution in KOKURA; partial cancellation, double speed and real hardware are not tested.

kitaqgb/kitaqgb/CodeGenerator.cs:1809

__cgb_safe_set_hdma4

Write HDMA4 ($FF54), the low byte of the VRAM destination address. Bits 0–3 are ignored, so the destination is aligned downward to 16 bytes. HDMA3=$E0, HDMA4=$1F selects $8010. This sets an address, not a transfer length.

Syntax

c
void __cgb_safe_set_hdma4(u8 value);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

Register value as u8 (0–255). The bit meanings for this particular register are given above; the function accepts a byte, not a pointer or a byte count.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Writes are guarded by the compiler's CGB-mode check and do nothing in DMG mode. Safe denotes this mode guard only. The call does not save or restore the previous register value.

One block is 16 bytes. New transfers support 1–128 blocks (16–2048 bytes), so control value 0 means 16 bytes, not an empty transfer. GDMA pauses the CPU until complete. HBlank DMA transfers one block per visible-line HBlank and runs across frames; the caller must poll or otherwise track completion before reusing its data.

Use GDMA with the LCD off or within a sufficiently long writable period. Start HBlank DMA with the LCD running, outside STAT mode 0; this sample starts it in VBlank. Do not change source banks, destination VBK or the source data while it is active. HALT pauses HBlank DMA too. No wait, interrupt protection or source/destination range check is added by these setters.

Read $FF55 separately: bit 7 clear means active; $FF means completed. After cancellation, bit 7 is set and bits 0–6 retain remaining blocks minus one. Cancellation does not undo copied bytes. Only issue a cancel command while a transfer is active; otherwise bit 7 clear starts GDMA. Keep the entire transfer inside valid source memory and the destination VRAM bank; do not rely on address overflow.

These HDMA register setters control CGB hardware DMA. __vram_copy_dma is a separate compiler alias for a CPU copy loop; its name does not make it use this DMA controller.

Usage example

c
__cgb_safe_set_hdma4(0x1F); // Destination aligns to $8010, not $801F.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

The complete program compares three transfer paths using a 16-byte-aligned buffer at $C600. GDMA copies 32 bytes to $8010: at screen (16,24) a red 8 × 8 triangle grows from one leftmost pixel to a full bottom row, followed at (24,24) by a red one-pixel square outline. HBlank DMA copies 32 bytes to $8030: at (16,48) a blue 4 × 8 left half-tile is followed at (24,48) by a blue 8 × 8 square. These are actual transferred tile patterns, not numerical placeholders.

The CANCELED row contains a green 4 × 8 half-tile at (16,72) and a green 8 × 8 square at (24,72), initially written by the CPU. A two-block HBlank transfer targeting these tiles is armed and canceled in the same VBlank, before its first block; $FF55 must read $81, and both patterns must remain unchanged after a frame. On DMG, the first two rows stay blank and only these two sentinels appear in black. Text remains black on white. FAILED CHECKS 000 also checks copied bytes and completion status. The test covers dual-mode DMG/CGB and CGB-only execution in KOKURA; partial cancellation, double speed and real hardware are not tested.

__cgb_safe_set_hdma5

Write HDMA5 ($FF55) to start DMA, or cancel an active HBlank transfer. For a new transfer, bits 0–6 encode block count minus one and bit 7 chooses GDMA (0) or HBlank DMA (1). During an active HBlank transfer, writing bit 7 clear cancels the remaining work; it does not start a new GDMA transfer.

Syntax

c

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

__cgb_safe_set_obpd

Write one byte to the sprite (OBJ) palette data register OCPD/OBPD at $FF6B on CGB. __cgb_safe_set_ocpd is another name for the same register. The associated index register selects the byte position; a full RGB15 color requires a low byte and a high byte.

Syntax

c
__cgb_safe_set_obpd(/* 書式は実装と呼び出し例を参照 */);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

One byte, 0–255. Write the low byte followed by the high byte of RGB15; enable index auto-increment first, or explicitly select each position. A data write does not enable auto-increment itself.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Palette and guarded register writes do nothing in DMG mode. Here, safe means a CGB-mode guard, not a PPU access wait or a critical section. Use LCD-off setup or a sufficiently short update in an accessible PPU phase. These functions do not wait for VBlank or protect a multi-byte update from interrupts.

Usage example

c
__cgb_safe_set_obpd(0); __cgb_safe_set_obpd(0x7C); // Alias of OCPD: blue at slot 31.
expect_color(1,31,31744);

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This program teaches how palette contents affect the same tile pattern differently in BG and OBJ. Each triangle is 8 × 8 pixels; its top row has one pixel on the left, expanding to a full bottom row. At x = 16, 32, 48, 64, 80, 96, 112, the BG row at y = 24 is red, blue, green, red, blue, green, red. The OBJ row at y = 64 is blue, green, red, blue, green, red, blue. The columns use single-color, component, indexed-range, full-palette, absolute-range, canonical-register and alias-register writes, respectively.

At y = 88, the seven 8 × 8 cells show: red triangle (x = 16), blue one-pixel square outline from bank 1 (32), horizontally reflected green triangle (48), vertically reflected red triangle (64), blue triangle reflected both ways (80), green triangle over a red sprite with red visible through color-zero pixels (96), and a full red sprite in front of the BG triangle as a priority control (112). Coordinates are screen pixels measured from the upper-left corner. Blank space and black text remain on white.

FAILED CHECKS 000 confirms RGB masking, truth-valued flags (including input 2), palette index wrapping, writes crossing a palette boundary, clamping at slot 31, null/zero-length no-ops and palette readback while LCD is off. Four target/hardware combinations are tested: dual-mode ROM on DMG and CGB, DMG-only on DMG, and CGB-only on CGB. On DMG, CGB MODE is 0, all triangles are black, the bank/flip attributes have no display effect, and the last two bottom cells are black squares. CGB MODE is 1 in CGB tests. Real hardware and LCD-on palette contention are not covered.

__cgb_safe_set_obpi

Write the sprite (OBJ) palette byte-index register OCPS/OBPI at $FF6A on CGB. __cgb_safe_set_ocps writes the same register. This selects where a subsequent data-register access goes; it does not itself write a color.

Syntax

c
__cgb_safe_set_obpi(/* 書式は実装と呼び出し例を参照 */);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

Byte index in bits 0–5 (0–63), with bit 7 set for auto-increment after data writes; bit 6 is unused. Color slot n starts at byte 2n. Auto-increment wraps after byte 63, so raw register writes require your own range control.

Return value

__cgb_safe_set_svbk

Write the raw WRAM selector SVBK ($FF70) on CGB. Hardware uses bits 0–2; raw values 0 and 1 both map bank 1 at $D000–$DFFF. For example, 8 also maps bank 1. Unlike __svbk_set, this guarded setter permits a dual-mode target and does not return the previous bank.

Syntax

c
void __cgb_safe_set_svbk(u8 value);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

value

Register value as u8 (0–255). The bit meanings for this particular register are given above; the function accepts a byte, not a pointer or a byte count.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Writes are guarded by the compiler's CGB-mode check and do nothing in DMG mode. Safe denotes this mode guard only. The call does not save or restore the previous register value.

Switching banks changes every access in $D000–$DFFF; fixed WRAM at $C000–$CFFF stays mapped. Keep the stack and all state needed during the switch outside the switched window. The sample uses --stack-bank=fixed and explicitly located fixed-WRAM state. Coordinate interrupts and banked DMA with the switch; these APIs add no critical section.

Usage example

c
__cgb_safe_set_svbk(0); // Raw selector 0 maps effective bank 1; this API returns void.
if ((W_SELECTOR&7)!=0) failures++;

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

The complete CGB-only program stores 1 at $D100 in WRAM bank 2 and 2 at the same address in bank 3, then reads both back and restores bank 1. The values choose an 8 × 8 red square at (16,24) and a blue one-pixel 8 × 8 square outline at (16,48). FAILED CHECKS 000 checks raw-selector normalization, bank separation and the previous-bank return for constant, variable, arithmetic and function-result arguments. Six separate invalid-build fixtures verify KQ2103 for DMG/dual targets and KQ2102 for constant banks 0 and 8. Runtime verification uses KOKURA; real hardware and interrupt-driven concurrent bank changes are not tested.

    AsmOperand win_done  = MakeUniqueLabel("getwinbase_done");

    EmitAsm("LDH_A_MEM", new AsmOperand(0x40, AddressMode.HighMem)); // LCDC
    EmitAsm("AND_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x40, AddressMode.Immediate)); // bit6 (Window tile map display select)
    EmitAsm("JR_Z", win_base0);
    EmitAsm("LD_HL_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x9C00, AddressMode.Immediate16));
    EmitAsm("JR", win_done);
    EmitLabel(win_base0);
    EmitAsm("LD_HL_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x9800, AddressMode.Immediate16));
    EmitLabel(win_done);
    LastCallReturnsHL = true;
    return;
}

Declaration or identifier source: kitaqgb/kitaqgb/CodeGenerator.cs:11320

__padrep

Schedule one repeating action selected by mask. Among newly pressed eligible bits, choose the lowest set bit and emit it immediately. Otherwise keep the active action while it remains eligible and held. If that action ends, adopt the lowest remaining held bit without an immediate pulse. No eligible held bits clears the runtime state.

Syntax

c
u8 __padrep(u8* state, u8 keys, u8 trigger, u8 mask);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

state

Pointer to five writable, consecutive RAM bytes, for example u8 state[5]. The layout is [0] active button bit, [1] initial delay, [2] repeat interval, [3] elapsed initial-delay count, [4] elapsed repeat count. Allocate a separate state for each independent scheduler. Initialize it before use; a null pointer, fewer than five bytes, or sharing the same state between directional and general schedulers is not supported.

keys

Current full held-button mask in the GB BTN_* layout. Supply a snapshot from __readpad, the low byte of __readpadex, or input_current; the scheduler does not poll hardware itself.

trigger

Newly pressed bits for this update in the same layout as keys, normally keys & ~previous_keys or the high byte of __readpadex. Only include bits also present in keys. Passing the held mask every time falsely restarts the initial press on every call.

mask

Eligible GB button bits combined with |. Zero disables all actions and clears the runtime state. Multiple bits enable several candidates, but the function emits only one selected bit per call, not a combined pulse mask.

Return value

Zero when no action pulse is due; otherwise one of 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128. The nonzero result is a button bit, not a normalized Boolean or a count of repeats.

Conditions and limits

Shared masks: BTN_RIGHT=0x01, BTN_LEFT=0x02, BTN_UP=0x04, BTN_DOWN=0x08, BTN_A=0x10, BTN_B=0x20, BTN_SELECT=0x40, BTN_START=0x80. A set bit means held. This layout applies to input_* on both GB and FC; raw FC pad functions use a different order.

Initial delay, 0–255 scheduler calls. With a positive value N, the first held repeat occurs exactly N calls after the initial press pulse. With 0, the initial-delay equality case is skipped and the repeat interval determines when the first held pulse occurs. These counts are not milliseconds.

Interval for subsequent repeats, 0–255 calls. Positive N emits every N eligible held updates after the first repeat. Zero emits on every eligible held update after the initial delay, so 0 and 1 both give one pulse per update at that stage. With both delay and interval set to 0, each held update emits a pulse.

Call the scheduler once per intended input tick. It modifies the state on every call and does not wait for VBlank. If a held button is adopted without a new-press bit, that adoption call returns 0 and starts a fresh delay. These functions schedule one active action per state; input_repeat instead maintains independent timers for all eight buttons.

Usage example

c
result=__padrep(actions,keys,trigger,48); // A|B: select A, the lower bit.
    if (result!=0) action_pulses++;

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This program feeds controlled button snapshots into four independent states to show exactly when a repeat fires. With delay 3 and interval 2, eight calls numbered 0–7 emit pulses on calls 0, 3, 5 and 7. RIGHT PULSES, DOWN PULSES, ACTION PULSES and ALIAS PULSES therefore each display 004. The general schedulers receive A and B together and select A (16). The program also checks initialization, reset, two-direction priority, release, adoption of an already held direction, zero timing values and an expression argument. CHECKS displays 057 and FAILED CHECKS must display 000. This is a deterministic state-processing example; it does not wait for you to press buttons.

Complete example program

Build command

powershell
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force .\out | Out-Null
.\kitaqgb\kitaqgb.exe .\kitaq-docs\samples\api-examples\gb\pad_repeat_state.c -I .\kitaq-docs\samples -I .\kitaqgb\lib -o .\out\pad_repeat_state.gb --profile=dev --rst-disable --stack-bank=fixed --cgb=cgb --no-cache --no-disasm

    AsmOperand pm_ret0        = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_ret0");
    AsmOperand pm_no_trig     = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_notrig");
    AsmOperand pm_reset_all   = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_reset");
    AsmOperand pm_dir0        = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_dir0");
    AsmOperand pm_have_dir    = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_havedir");
    AsmOperand pm_first_rep   = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_firstrep");
    AsmOperand pm_ret_dir     = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_retdir");
    AsmOperand pm_skip_dasinc = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_skipdasinc");
    AsmOperand pm_skip_arrinc = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_skiparrinc");

    // Evaluate args safely:
    // HL = state*, B = keys, C = trigger, E = mask
    CompileIntoHL(args[0]);
    EmitAsm("PUSH_HL");
    CompileIntoA(args[1]); EmitAsm("LD_B_A"); // keys
    CompileIntoA(args[2]); EmitAsm("LD_C_A"); // trigger
    CompileIntoA(args[3]); EmitAsm("LD_E_A"); // mask
    EmitAsm("POP_HL");

    // trigMasked = trigger & mask -> D
    EmitAsm("LD_A_C");
    EmitAsm("AND_E");
    EmitAsm("LD_D_A");
    EmitAsm("OR_A");
    EmitAsm("JP_Z", pm_no_trig);

    // select = lowbit(trigMasked) = x & -x
    EmitAsm("LD_A_D");
    EmitAsm("CPL");
    EmitAsm("INC_A");
    EmitAsm("AND_D");
    EmitAsm("LD_C_A"); // save selected in C

    // dirMask=selected, dasCnt=0, arrCnt=0
    EmitAsm("LD_A_C");
    EmitAsm("LD_HL_A"); // [0]=selected
    EmitAsm("PUSH_HL");
    EmitAsm("INC_HL"); Emi
__padrep_down

Advance a repeat action for Down only. A new Down press returns 8 immediately and restarts the counters. Held Down produces later pulses according to the state's timing; releasing Down clears the runtime state. Other buttons do not trigger this scheduler.

Syntax

c
u8 __padrep_down(u8* state, u8 keys, u8 trigger);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

state

Pointer to five writable, consecutive RAM bytes, for example u8 state[5]. The layout is [0] active button bit, [1] initial delay, [2] repeat interval, [3] elapsed initial-delay count, [4] elapsed repeat count. Allocate a separate state for each independent scheduler. Initialize it before use; a null pointer, fewer than five bytes, or sharing the same state between directional and general schedulers is not supported.

keys

trigger

Return value

Zero when no action pulse is due; otherwise one of 8. The nonzero result is a button bit, not a normalized Boolean or a count of repeats.

__padrep_init

Initialize a caller-owned five-byte repeat state: clear the active action and both counters, and store the supplied initial delay and repeat interval. No buttons are read and no repeat pulse is emitted.

Syntax

c
void __padrep_init(u8* state, u8 das, u8 arr);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

state

Pointer to five writable, consecutive RAM bytes, for example u8 state[5]. The layout is [0] active button bit, [1] initial delay, [2] repeat interval, [3] elapsed initial-delay count, [4] elapsed repeat count. Allocate a separate state for each independent scheduler. Initialize it before use; a null pointer, fewer than five bytes, or sharing the same state between directional and general schedulers is not supported.

das

Initial delay, 0–255 scheduler calls. With a positive value N, the first held repeat occurs exactly N calls after the initial press pulse. With 0, the initial-delay equality case is skipped and the repeat interval determines when the first held pulse occurs. These counts are not milliseconds.

arr

Interval for subsequent repeats, 0–255 calls. Positive N emits every N eligible held updates after the first repeat. Zero emits on every eligible held update after the initial delay, so 0 and 1 both give one pulse per update at that stage. With both delay and interval set to 0, each held update emits a pulse.

Return value

None (void).

Usage example

c
__padrep_init(lr,3,2);
// Active action and counters are zero; delay and interval are retained.
expect(lr[0],0); expect(lr[1],3); expect(lr[2],2);
expect(lr[3],0); expect(lr[4],0);

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This program feeds controlled button snapshots into four independent states to show exactly when a repeat fires. With delay 3 and interval 2, eight calls numbered 0–7 emit pulses on calls 0, 3, 5 and 7. RIGHT PULSES, DOWN PULSES, ACTION PULSES and ALIAS PULSES therefore each display 004. The general schedulers receive A and B together and select A (16). The program also checks initialization, reset, two-direction priority, release, adoption of an already held direction, zero timing values and an expression argument. CHECKS displays 057 and FAILED CHECKS must display 000. This is a deterministic state-processing example; it does not wait for you to press buttons.

// 2.21 __padrep_reset(state_ptr) -> void
// Resets runtime counters for PadRepeatState while keeping das/arr params:
// dirMask=0, dasCnt=0, arrCnt=0
// Treated as a compiler intrinsic (no C definition required).
if (funcName == "__padrep_reset")
{
    if (args.Length != 1) Error(expr, "__padrep_reset requires 1 argument (state*)");

    CompileIntoHL(args[0]);

    EmitAsm("XOR_A");
    EmitAsm("LD_HL_A"); // [0]=0 (dirMask)

    // [3]=0, [4]=0
    EmitAsm("PUSH_HL");
    EmitAsm("INC_HL"); EmitAsm("INC_HL"); EmitAsm("INC_HL"); // -> [3]
    EmitAsm("LD_HL_A");
    EmitAsm("INC_HL"); // -> [4]
    EmitAsm("LD_HL_A");
    EmitAsm("POP_HL");
    return;
}

// __padrep_lr(state*, keys, trigger) returns Right (1), Left (2), or zero.
// New triggers take priority, with Right selected first when both trigger bits are set.
// Without a new
__padrep_lr

Advance a single Left/Right repeat action. A new Right press emits 1 immediately; otherwise a new Left press emits 2. If both are newly pressed, Right wins. Without a new press, holding both directions returns 0 and freezes the counters; holding neither clears the runtime state.

Syntax

c
u8 __padrep_lr(u8* state, u8 keys, u8 trigger);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

state

Pointer to five writable, consecutive RAM bytes, for example u8 state[5]. The layout is [0] active button bit, [1] initial delay, [2] repeat interval, [3] elapsed initial-delay count, [4] elapsed repeat count. Allocate a separate state for each independent scheduler. Initialize it before use; a null pointer, fewer than five bytes, or sharing the same state between directional and general schedulers is not supported.

keys

Current full held-button mask in the GB BTN_* layout. Supply a snapshot from __readpad, the low byte of __readpadex, or input_current; the scheduler does not poll hardware itself.

trigger

Newly pressed bits for this update in the same layout as keys, normally keys & ~previous_keys or the high byte of __readpadex. Only include bits also present in keys. Passing the held mask every time falsely restarts the initial press on every call.

Return value

Zero when no action pulse is due; otherwise one of 1, 2. The nonzero result is a button bit, not a normalized Boolean or a count of repeats.

Conditions and limits

Shared masks: BTN_RIGHT=0x01, BTN_LEFT=0x02, BTN_UP=0x04, BTN_DOWN=0x08, BTN_A=0x10, BTN_B=0x20, BTN_SELECT=0x40, BTN_START=0x80. A set bit means held. This layout applies to input_* on both GB and FC; raw FC pad functions use a different order.

Initial delay, 0–255 scheduler calls. With a positive value N, the first held repeat occurs exactly N calls after the initial press pulse. With 0, the initial-delay equality case is skipped and the repeat interval determines when the first held pulse occurs. These counts are not milliseconds.

Interval for subsequent repeats, 0–255 calls. Positive N emits every N eligible held updates after the first repeat. Zero emits on every eligible held update after the initial delay, so 0 and 1 both give one pulse per update at that stage. With both delay and interval set to 0, each held update emits a pulse.

Call the scheduler once per intended input tick. It modifies the state on every call and does not wait for VBlank. If a held button is adopted without a new-press bit, that adoption call returns 0 and starts a fresh delay. These functions schedule one active action per state; input_repeat instead maintains independent timers for all eight buttons.

Usage example

c
result=__padrep_lr(lr,keys,trigger);
    if (result!=0) pulses++;

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This program feeds controlled button snapshots into four independent states to show exactly when a repeat fires. With delay 3 and interval 2, eight calls numbered 0–7 emit pulses on calls 0, 3, 5 and 7. RIGHT PULSES, DOWN PULSES, ACTION PULSES and ALIAS PULSES therefore each display 004. The general schedulers receive A and B together and select A (16). The program also checks initialization, reset, two-direction priority, release, adoption of an already held direction, zero timing values and an expression argument. CHECKS displays 057 and FAILED CHECKS must display 000. This is a deterministic state-processing example; it does not wait for you to press buttons.

__padrep_mask

__padrep_mask is an alternative spelling of __padrep; both enter the same compiler implementation. It has the same single-action selection, timing, state layout and return values. The explicit name can make a masked action scheduler easier to recognize at a call site.

Schedule one repeating action selected by mask. Among newly pressed eligible bits, choose the lowest set bit and emit it immediately. Otherwise keep the active action while it remains eligible and held. If that action ends, adopt the lowest remaining held bit without an immediate pulse. No eligible held bits clears the runtime state.

Syntax

c
u8 __padrep_mask(u8* state, u8 keys, u8 trigger, u8 mask);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

state

Pointer to five writable, consecutive RAM bytes, for example u8 state[5]. The layout is [0] active button bit, [1] initial delay, [2] repeat interval, [3] elapsed initial-delay count, [4] elapsed repeat count. Allocate a separate state for each independent scheduler. Initialize it before use; a null pointer, fewer than five bytes, or sharing the same state between directional and general schedulers is not supported.

keys

Current full held-button mask in the GB BTN_* layout. Supply a snapshot from __readpad, the low byte of __readpadex, or input_current; the scheduler does not poll hardware itself.

trigger

Newly pressed bits for this update in the same layout as keys, normally keys & ~previous_keys or the high byte of __readpadex. Only include bits also present in keys. Passing the held mask every time falsely restarts the initial press on every call.

mask

Eligible GB button bits combined with |. Zero disables all actions and clears the runtime state. Multiple bits enable several candidates, but the function emits only one selected bit per call, not a combined pulse mask.

Return value

Zero when no action pulse is due; otherwise one of 1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128. The nonzero result is a button bit, not a normalized Boolean or a count of repeats.

Conditions and limits

Shared masks: BTN_RIGHT=0x01, BTN_LEFT=0x02, BTN_UP=0x04, BTN_DOWN=0x08, BTN_A=0x10, BTN_B=0x20, BTN_SELECT=0x40, BTN_START=0x80. A set bit means held. This layout applies to input_* on both GB and FC; raw FC pad functions use a different order.

Initial delay, 0–255 scheduler calls. With a positive value N, the first held repeat occurs exactly N calls after the initial press pulse. With 0, the initial-delay equality case is skipped and the repeat interval determines when the first held pulse occurs. These counts are not milliseconds.

Interval for subsequent repeats, 0–255 calls. Positive N emits every N eligible held updates after the first repeat. Zero emits on every eligible held update after the initial delay, so 0 and 1 both give one pulse per update at that stage. With both delay and interval set to 0, each held update emits a pulse.

Call the scheduler once per intended input tick. It modifies the state on every call and does not wait for VBlank. If a held button is adopted without a new-press bit, that adoption call returns 0 and starts a fresh delay. These functions schedule one active action per state; input_repeat instead maintains independent timers for all eight buttons.

Usage example

c
result=__padrep_mask(alias_state,keys,trigger,48);
    if (result!=0) alias_pulses++;

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This program feeds controlled button snapshots into four independent states to show exactly when a repeat fires. With delay 3 and interval 2, eight calls numbered 0–7 emit pulses on calls 0, 3, 5 and 7. RIGHT PULSES, DOWN PULSES, ACTION PULSES and ALIAS PULSES therefore each display 004. The general schedulers receive A and B together and select A (16). The program also checks initialization, reset, two-direction priority, release, adoption of an already held direction, zero timing values and an expression argument. CHECKS displays 057 and FAILED CHECKS must display 000. This is a deterministic state-processing example; it does not wait for you to press buttons.

    AsmOperand pm_ret0        = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_ret0");
    AsmOperand pm_no_trig     = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_notrig");
    AsmOperand pm_reset_all   = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_reset");
    AsmOperand pm_dir0        = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_dir0");
    AsmOperand pm_have_dir    = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_havedir");
    AsmOperand pm_first_rep   = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_firstrep");
    AsmOperand pm_ret_dir     = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_retdir");
    AsmOperand pm_skip_dasinc = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_skipdasinc");
    AsmOperand pm_skip_arrinc = MakeUniqueLabel("padrepM_skiparrinc");

    // Evaluate args safely:
    // HL = state*, B = keys, C = trigger, E = mask
    CompileIntoHL(args[0]);
    EmitAsm("PUSH_HL");
    CompileIntoA(args[1]); EmitAsm("LD_B_A"); // keys

    EmitAsm("POP_HL");

    // trigMasked = trigger & mask -> D
    EmitAsm("LD_A_C");
    EmitAsm("AND_E");
    EmitAsm("LD_D_A");
    EmitAsm("OR_A");
    EmitAsm("JP_Z", pm_no_trig);

    // select = lowbit(trigMasked) = x & -x
    EmitAsm("LD_A_D");
    EmitAsm("CPL");
    EmitAsm("INC_A");
    EmitAsm("AND_D");
    EmitAsm("LD_C_A"); // save selected in C

    // dirMask=selected, dasCnt=0, arrCnt=0
    EmitAsm("LD_A_C");
    EmitAsm("LD_HL_A"); // [0]=selected
    EmitAsm("PUSH_HL");
    EmitAsm("INC_HL"); Emi
__padrep_reset

Cancel the active action and clear both elapsed counters while preserving the configured delay and interval. Use this when closing a menu or changing input focus so a previous hold does not carry its repeat timing into the next interaction.

Syntax

c
void __padrep_reset(u8* state);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

state

Pointer to five writable, consecutive RAM bytes, for example u8 state[5]. The layout is [0] active button bit, [1] initial delay, [2] repeat interval, [3] elapsed initial-delay count, [4] elapsed repeat count. Allocate a separate state for each independent scheduler. Initialize it before use; a null pointer, fewer than five bytes, or sharing the same state between directional and general schedulers is not supported.

Return value

None (void).

Usage example

expect(lr[0],0); expect(lr[1],3); expect(lr[2],2);
expect(lr[3],0); expect(lr[4],0);

// __padrep_lr(state*, keys, trigger) returns Right (1), Left (2), or zero.
// New triggers take priority, with Right selected first when both trigger bits are set.
// Without a new trigger, simultaneous Left/Right holds suppress movement without resetting counters.
// A single held direction is adopted without immediate movement when no direction is active.
// Subsequent updates use the five-byte state's initial delay and repeat interval; ARR=0 repeats each update.

__readpad

Read directions and action buttons from JOYP and combine them into the shared 8-bit button mask. Selects each JOYP group, performs two reads for settling, then restores the group-selection bits to 0x30.

Syntax

c
u8 __readpad();

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Return value

An 8-bit mask in the shared BTN_* layout. Several buttons may be set simultaneously; 0 means none.

Conditions and limits

Shared masks: BTN_RIGHT=0x01, BTN_LEFT=0x02, BTN_UP=0x04, BTN_DOWN=0x08, BTN_A=0x10, BTN_B=0x20, BTN_SELECT=0x40, BTN_START=0x80. A set bit means held. This layout applies to input_* on both GB and FC; raw FC pad functions use a different order.

This call reads a fresh hardware snapshot; it does not update input_* or nes_pad1_* cached state and does not generate pressed/released edges or repeats. Read once, save the result, and derive edges against your saved previous mask when needed.

Usage example

c
all_keys=__readpad(); // A + Right: 0x11, decimal 17.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

Use this program to compare the three button-mask layouts and the packed press result. Hold A and Right together. ALL KEYS is 17; DIRECTIONS and BUTTONS LOW are each 1. With a previous mask of 0, EX CURRENT and EX NEW PRESS are both 17. Passing the current mask to the next read makes HELD PRESS 0. FAILED CHECKS must be 0. The values remain on screen after the snapshot, so releasing the buttons does not erase them.

Complete example program

Build command

powershell
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force .\out | Out-Null
.\kitaqgb\kitaqgb.exe .\kitaq-docs\samples\api-examples\gb\input_raw.c -I .\kitaqgb\lib -I .\kitaq-docs\samples -o .\out\input_raw.gb --profile=dev --rst-disable --stack-bank=fixed --cgb=cgb --no-disasm --no-cache

Implementation and source

kitaqgb/kitaqgb/CodeGenerator.cs:10537

csharp
{
    if (args.Length != 0) Error(expr, "__readpad requires 0 arguments");

    AsmOperand p1 = new AsmOperand(0x00, AddressMode.HighMem); // P1 / JOYP

    // Directions (P1=0x20)
    EmitAsm("LD_A_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x20, AddressMode.Immediate));
    EmitAsm("LDH_MEM_A", p1);
    EmitAsm("LDH_A_MEM", p1); // dummy reads for settle
    EmitAsm("LDH_A_MEM", p1);
    EmitAsm("CPL");
    EmitAsm("AND_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x0F, AddressMode.Immediate));
    EmitAsm("LD_C_A"); // dpad in C

    // Buttons (P1=0x10)
    EmitAsm("LD_A_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x10, AddressMode.Immediate));
    EmitAsm("LDH_MEM_A", p1);
    EmitAsm("LDH_A_MEM", p1);
    EmitAsm("LDH_A_MEM", p1);
    EmitAsm("CPL");
    EmitAsm("AND_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x0F, AddressMode.Immediate));
    // shift into upper nibble (<<4)
    EmitAsm("RLCA"); EmitAsm("RLCA"); EmitAsm("RLCA"); EmitAsm("RLCA");
    EmitAsm("OR_C");

    // Restore P1 selection (0x30) without losing return value
    EmitAsm("LD_C_A");
    EmitAsm("LD_A_IMM", new AsmOperand(0x30, AddressMode.Immediate));
    EmitAsm("LDH_MEM_A", p1);
    EmitAsm("LD_A_C");
    return;
}

__readpadbtn

Read only the JOYP action-button group as a low nibble: A=1, B=2, Select=4, Start=8. These values are not BTN_A through BTN_START; those shared masks occupy the high nibble. JOYP selection is restored to 0x30.

Syntax

c
u8 __readpadbtn();

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Return value

A mask from 0 to 15 in the bit order described above. More than one bit can be set; test a button with result & bit, rather than comparing the whole result to 1.

Conditions and limits

This call reads a fresh hardware snapshot; it does not update input_* or nes_pad1_* cached state and does not generate pressed/released edges or repeats. Read once, save the result, and derive edges against your saved previous mask when needed.

__sdot2_q1_7Compiler intrinsic

Perform the operation specified by the arguments. Module: Compiler intrinsics. The declaration, original notes and implementation below define exact units, terminators and return conditions.

c
__sdot2_q1_7(arg0, arg1, arg2, arg3);

Declaration or identifier source: kitaqgb/kitaqgb/CodeGenerator.cs:11621

__settile_fast

Write one tile-number byte to the tile map; this selects an existing tile image, not new pixel data.

The destination is always 0x9800 + 32*y + x, regardless of which maps LCDC selects for the background and window.

Does not wait for VRAM access. Call only while the LCD is off or within a caller-managed writable interval with enough time for the whole transfer.

Syntax

c
__settile_fast(x, y, tile);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

x, y

Coordinates in 8×8 tiles, each from 0 to 31. Keep the complete write inside the 32×32 map; coordinates are not clipped.

tile

Tile number, 0–255. LCDC bit 4 determines how the number addresses tile-image data. It is not a VRAM byte address.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Tile bytes go to the currently selected VRAM bank. On CGB, select bank 0 for tile numbers; this call does not switch banks for you.

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

Usage example

c
// __settile_fast
// Write one tile-number byte to the tile map; this selects an existing tile image, not
// new pixel data. The destination is always `0x9800 + 32*y + x`, regardless of which
// maps LCDC selects for the background and window.
// Expected: FAILED CHECKS 000, with the written tile data in the selected map.
#include "gb_tile_color_example.h"
void __settile_fast(u8 x, u8 y, u8 tile);

void main() {
    u8 failures;
    failures = 0;
    tile_color_example_begin();
    __settile_fast(2, 3, 'A');
    if (tile_example_read(0x9862, 0) != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C62, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    tile_example_finish(failures, 0);
}

Expected result

The selected map shows the written characters or filled area. The program reads back the addressed tile bytes and, on CGB, the attribute bytes; FAILED CHECKS 000 means those comparisons passed. Both maps initially contain zero, and the example selects 0x9C00 for BG/window before the demonstrated call, so a fixed-map call can be distinguished from a selected-map call. These examples perform the writes with the LCD off; they do not measure LCD-on timing.

On CGB, the sample characters and status text are blue on white. The setup gives palette 0 a blue ink color; it does not change the tile-map attributes. On DMG, the same characters are black on white.

Expected image: the following tile pattern starts at tile coordinates (x=2, y=3), or pixel coordinates (16,24). Each character occupies one 8×8 tile. Read the pattern from top to bottom and left to right; the surrounding tiles remain blank.

text
A

                // Evaluate args left-to-right (matches normal call semantics)
                // Preserve x,y while evaluating tile.
                CompileIntoA(args[0]);
                EmitAsm("LD_L_A");
                EmitAsm("LD_H_IMM", new AsmOperand(0, AddressMode.Immediate));
                EmitAsm("PUSH_HL"); // x

                CompileIntoA(args[1]);
                EmitAsm("LD_L_A");
                EmitAsm("LD_H_IMM", new AsmOperand(0, AddressMode.Immediate));
                EmitAsm("PUSH_HL"); // y

                CompileIntoA(args[2]);
                EmitAsm("LD_C_A"); // tile

                // Restore y -> A, x -> B
                EmitAsm("POP_HL");
                EmitAsm("LD_A_L");

                EmitAsm("POP_HL");
                EmitAsm("LD_A_L");
                EmitAsm("LD_B_A"); // x

                EmitAsm("LD_A_D"); // y

__settilebg16_buf

Write a 16×16-pixel cell into a RAM tile-map buffer: the four 8×8 tile numbers are tile,tile+1 on top and tile+2,tile+3 below. Values wrap at 256. This does not transfer anything to VRAM.

Syntax

c
__settilebg16_buf(buf, x, y, tile);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

buf

A RAM buffer with 32 bytes per tile row. A full 32×32 map needs 1024 bytes. The upper-left byte is at 64*y + 2*x; the four offsets are 0,1,32,33 from there.

x, y

Coordinates in 16×16-pixel cells, each from 0 to 15 for a full 32×32-tile buffer. These differ from the 8×8-tile coordinates used by scalar setters.

tile

Tile number, 0–255. LCDC bit 4 determines how the number addresses tile-image data. It is not a VRAM byte address.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

Usage example

c
// __settilebg16_buf
// Write a 16×16-pixel cell into a RAM tile-map buffer: the four 8×8 tile numbers are
// `tile,tile+1` on top and `tile+2,tile+3` below. Values wrap at 256. This does not
// transfer anything to VRAM.
// Expected: FAILED CHECKS 000, with the written tile data in the selected map.
#include "gb_tile_color_example.h"
u8 example_tiles[1024];
u8 example_attrs[1024];
void __memset(void* dest, u8 value, u16 len);
void __settilebg16_buf(u8* buf, u8 x, u8 y, u8 tile);
void __settilebg16cgb_buf(u8* tilebuf, u8* attrbuf, u8 x, u8 y, u8 tile, u8 attr);
void __settilebg16_flush(const u8* buf, u8 y, u8 x, u8 count);
void __settilebg16cgb_flush(const u8* tilebuf, const u8* attrbuf, u8 y, u8 x, u8 count);

void main() {
    u8 failures;
    failures = 0;
    tile_color_example_begin();
    __memset(example_tiles, 0, 1024);
    __memset(example_attrs, 0, 1024);
    __settilebg16_buf(example_tiles, 1, 1, 'A');
    // The buffer operation must not write to VRAM yet.
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C42, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    // Transfer the prepared cell so its four characters can be seen.
    __settilebg16_flush(example_tiles, 1, 2, 2);
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C42, 0) != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C43, 0) != 'B') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C62, 0) != 'C') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C63, 0) != 'D') { failures++; }
    if (example_tiles[66] != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (example_tiles[99] != 'D') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C44, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    tile_example_finish(failures, 1);
}

Expected result

The selected map shows the written characters or filled area. The program reads back the addressed tile bytes and, on CGB, the attribute bytes; FAILED CHECKS 000 means those comparisons passed. Both maps initially contain zero, and the example selects 0x9C00 for BG/window before the demonstrated call, so a fixed-map call can be distinguished from a selected-map call. These examples perform the writes with the LCD off; they do not measure LCD-on timing.

The visible cell contains AB above CD, at tile coordinates (2,2). This is one 16×16-pixel cell at cell coordinates (1,1). Buffer examples first verify that RAM preparation leaves VRAM unchanged, then flush the cell; flush examples prepare the RAM bytes explicitly before transferring them.

On CGB, the sample characters and status text are blue on white. The setup gives palette 0 a blue ink color; it does not change the tile-map attributes. On DMG, the same characters are black on white.

Expected image: the following tile pattern starts at tile coordinates (x=2, y=2), or pixel coordinates (16,16). Each character occupies one 8×8 tile. Read the pattern from top to bottom and left to right; the surrounding tiles remain blank.

__settilebg16_flush

Transfer two adjacent tile rows from the RAM buffer to the LCDC-selected BG map. The source offset is 64*y + x; count bytes are copied from that row and from the row 32 bytes below it.

Waits for writable VRAM when necessary. The LCD-on path uses interrupt masking and can enable interrupts afterward; it is not a promise to preserve the caller's interrupt state or to finish within one VBlank.

Syntax

c
__settilebg16_flush(buf, y, x, count);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

buf

Readable tile-map buffer with a 32-byte row stride. A full map occupies 1024 bytes. The two-buffer form takes separate tile-number and attribute arrays with the same layout; it does not interleave them.

y, x, count

Argument order is y,x,count. y is a 16-pixel cell row (0–15); x and count are 8-pixel tile units. Keep x+count <= 32. For one cell at (2,3), use y=3,x=4,count=2.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Tile bytes go to the currently selected VRAM bank. On CGB, select bank 0 for tile numbers; this call does not switch banks for you.

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

Usage example

            // Flush the tile buffer first and copy attributes only when CGB hardware is available.
            if (funcName == "__settilebg16cgb_flush")
            {
                if (args.Length != 5) Error(expr, "__settilebg16cgb_flush requires 5 arguments");
                EmitTile16FlushRowsIntrinsic(args[0], args[2], args[3], args[4], cgbAttr: false, labelPrefix: "settilebg16cgbflush_tile");
                if (IsKnownCgbRuntimeTrue())
                {
                    EmitSetVbkUnchecked(1);
                    EmitTile16FlushRowsIntrinsic(args[1], args[2], args[3], args[4], cgbAttr: true, labelPrefix: "settilebg16cgbflush_attr");
                }
                else
                {
                    AsmOperand skip = MakeUniqueLabel("settilebg16cgbflush_skip");
                    AsmOperand done = MakeUniqueLabel("settilebg16cgbflush_done");
                    EmitRuntimeCgbCheckCall();
                    EmitAsm("OR_A");
                    // Two unrolled row copies can exceed the relative-branch range.
                    EmitAsm("JP_Z", skip);
                    EmitSetVbkUnchecked(1);
                    EmitTile16FlushRowsIntrinsic(args[1], args[2], args[3], args[4], cgbAttr: true, labelPrefix: "settilebg16cgbflush_attr");
                    EmitAsm("JR", done);
__settilebg16cgb_buf

Write a 16×16-pixel cell into a RAM tile-map buffer: the four 8×8 tile numbers are tile,tile+1 on top and tile+2,tile+3 below. Values wrap at 256. This does not transfer anything to VRAM.

Also write the same attr byte to all four corresponding cells in the separate attribute buffer. Both buffers are ordinary RAM, so this preparation also runs on DMG.

Syntax

c
__settilebg16cgb_buf(tilebuf, attrbuf, x, y, tile, attr);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

tilebuf, attrbuf

A RAM buffer with 32 bytes per tile row. A full 32×32 map needs 1024 bytes. The upper-left byte is at 64*y + 2*x; the four offsets are 0,1,32,33 from there.

x, y

Coordinates in 16×16-pixel cells, each from 0 to 15 for a full 32×32-tile buffer. These differ from the 8×8-tile coordinates used by scalar setters.

tile

Tile number, 0–255. LCDC bit 4 determines how the number addresses tile-image data. It is not a VRAM byte address.

attr

CGB attribute byte: bits 0–2 select palette 0–7; bit 3 selects tile-data bank; bits 5 and 6 flip horizontally and vertically; bit 7 sets BG/window priority. Keep unused bit 4 zero.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

Usage example

c
// __settilebg16cgb_buf
// Write a 16×16-pixel cell into a RAM tile-map buffer: the four 8×8 tile numbers are
// `tile,tile+1` on top and `tile+2,tile+3` below. Values wrap at 256. This does not
// transfer anything to VRAM. Also write the same `attr` byte to all four corresponding
// cells in the separate attribute buffer. Both buffers are ordinary RAM, so this
// preparation also runs on DMG.
// Expected: FAILED CHECKS 000, with the written tile data in the selected map.
#include "gb_tile_example.h"
u8 example_tiles[1024];
u8 example_attrs[1024];
void __memset(void* dest, u8 value, u16 len);
void __settilebg16_buf(u8* buf, u8 x, u8 y, u8 tile);
void __settilebg16cgb_buf(u8* tilebuf, u8* attrbuf, u8 x, u8 y, u8 tile, u8 attr);
void __settilebg16_flush(const u8* buf, u8 y, u8 x, u8 count);
void __settilebg16cgb_flush(const u8* tilebuf, const u8* attrbuf, u8 y, u8 x, u8 count);

void main() {
    u8 failures;
    failures = 0;
    tile_example_begin();
    __memset(example_tiles, 0, 1024);
    __memset(example_attrs, 0, 1024);
    __settilebg16cgb_buf(example_tiles, example_attrs, 1, 1, 'A', 2);
    // The buffer operation must not write to VRAM yet.
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C42, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    // Transfer the prepared cell so its four characters can be seen.
    __settilebg16cgb_flush(example_tiles, example_attrs, 1, 2, 2);
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C42, 0) != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C43, 0) != 'B') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C62, 0) != 'C') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C63, 0) != 'D') { failures++; }
    if (example_tiles[66] != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (example_tiles[99] != 'D') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C44, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    if (example_attrs[66] != 2) { failures++; }
    if (example_attrs[99] != 2) { failures++; }
    if (__cgb_is_cgb() && tile_example_read(0x9C42, 1) != 2) { failures++; }
    if (__cgb_is_cgb() && tile_example_read(0x9C63, 1) != 2) { failures++; }
    tile_example_finish(failures, 1);
}

Expected result

The selected map shows the written characters or filled area. The program reads back the addressed tile bytes and, on CGB, the attribute bytes; FAILED CHECKS 000 means those comparisons passed. Both maps initially contain zero, and the example selects 0x9C00 for BG/window before the demonstrated call, so a fixed-map call can be distinguished from a selected-map call. These examples perform the writes with the LCD off; they do not measure LCD-on timing.

The visible cell contains AB above CD, at tile coordinates (2,2). This is one 16×16-pixel cell at cell coordinates (1,1). Buffer examples first verify that RAM preparation leaves VRAM unchanged, then flush the cell; flush examples prepare the RAM bytes explicitly before transferring them.

All four characters are blue on CGB because the four attribute bytes are 2. They are black on DMG.

Expected image: the following tile pattern starts at tile coordinates (x=2, y=2), or pixel coordinates (16,16). Each character occupies one 8×8 tile. Read the pattern from top to bottom and left to right; the surrounding tiles remain blank.

text
AB
CD

__settilebg16cgb_flush

Transfer two adjacent tile rows from the RAM buffer to the LCDC-selected BG map. The source offset is 64*y + x; count bytes are copied from that row and from the row 32 bytes below it.

Write a tile number and its CGB attribute. On DMG, only the tile-number write is performed. On CGB, enter with VRAM bank 0 selected; attributes use bank 1 and the call finishes with VBK=0.

Waits for writable VRAM when necessary. The LCD-on path uses interrupt masking and can enable interrupts afterward; it is not a promise to preserve the caller's interrupt state or to finish within one VBlank.

Syntax

c
__settilebg16cgb_flush(tilebuf, attrbuf, y, x, count);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

tilebuf, attrbuf

Readable tile-map buffer with a 32-byte row stride. A full map occupies 1024 bytes. The two-buffer form takes separate tile-number and attribute arrays with the same layout; it does not interleave them.

y, x, count

Argument order is y,x,count. y is a 16-pixel cell row (0–15); x and count are 8-pixel tile units. Keep x+count <= 32. For one cell at (2,3), use y=3,x=4,count=2.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

                EmitAsm("LD_A_H");

                return;

                EmitAsm("LD_B_A");
                EmitLoadA(RngStateHiVar);
                EmitAsm("LD_C_A");

                AsmOperand rngSeeded = MakeUniqueL

Declaration or identifier source: kitaqgb/kitaqgb/CodeGenerator.cs:8714

__svbk_get

Read the effective WRAM bank mapped at $D000–$DFFF. The compiler reads SVBK bits 0–2 and converts raw selector 0 to bank 1. This returns a bank number, not RAM capacity, an address or a pointer.

Syntax

c
u8 __svbk_get();

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Return value

Current effective bank number as u8, 1–7; raw selector 0 is reported as 1.

Conditions and limits

__svbk_get and __svbk_set require --cgb=cgb_only or #pragma rom_cgb cgb_only. DMG and dual-mode builds reject them with KQ2103, even if a runtime condition would skip the call. Use the guarded raw setter only when its different contract fits your program.

Switching banks changes every access in $D000–$DFFF; fixed WRAM at $C000–$CFFF stays mapped. Keep the stack and all state needed during the switch outside the switched window. The sample uses --stack-bank=fixed and explicitly located fixed-WRAM state. Coordinate interrupts and banked DMA with the switch; these APIs add no critical section.

Usage example

c
if (__svbk_get()!=1) failures++; // Normalize raw selector zero to effective bank 1.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

The complete CGB-only program stores 1 at $D100 in WRAM bank 2 and 2 at the same address in bank 3, then reads both back and restores bank 1. The values choose an 8 × 8 red square at (16,24) and a blue one-pixel 8 × 8 square outline at (16,48). FAILED CHECKS 000 checks raw-selector normalization, bank separation and the previous-bank return for constant, variable, arithmetic and function-result arguments. Six separate invalid-build fixtures verify KQ2103 for DMG/dual targets and KQ2102 for constant banks 0 and 8. Runtime verification uses KOKURA; real hardware and interrupt-driven concurrent bank changes are not tested.

__svbk_set

Switch the WRAM bank mapped at $D000–$DFFF and return the effective bank selected before the switch. Save this return value if you need to restore the caller's bank. The memory contents are not copied or cleared, and the function does not restore the bank automatically.

Syntax

c
u8 __svbk_set(u8 bank);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

bank

Requested bank as u8, 1–7. Constant values outside this range produce KQ2102. Dynamic values are not range-checked by this intrinsic; validate them before calling rather than relying on register masking.

Return value

Effective bank number before the switch as u8, 1–7. This is the old bank, not the newly selected bank.

Conditions and limits

__svbk_get and __svbk_set require --cgb=cgb_only or #pragma rom_cgb cgb_only. DMG and dual-mode builds reject them with KQ2103, even if a runtime condition would skip the call. Use the guarded raw setter only when its different contract fits your program.

Switching banks changes every access in $D000–$DFFF; fixed WRAM at $C000–$CFFF stays mapped. Keep the stack and all state needed during the switch outside the switched window. The sample uses --stack-bank=fixed and explicitly located fixed-WRAM state. Coordinate interrupts and banked DMA with the switch; these APIs add no critical section.

Usage example

c
previous_bank=__svbk_set(requested_bank);
if(previous_bank!=1 || __svbk_get()!=2) failures++;
*((u8*)0xD100)=1; // This byte belongs to bank 2.
previous_bank=__svbk_set(next_bank()); // Dynamic argument evaluates to 3.
if(previous_bank!=2 || __svbk_get()!=3) failures++;
*((u8*)0xD100)=2; // Same CPU address, different physical RAM.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

The complete CGB-only program stores 1 at $D100 in WRAM bank 2 and 2 at the same address in bank 3, then reads both back and restores bank 1. The values choose an 8 × 8 red square at (16,24) and a blue one-pixel 8 × 8 square outline at (16,48). FAILED CHECKS 000 checks raw-selector normalization, bank separation and the previous-bank return for constant, variable, arithmetic and function-result arguments. Six separate invalid-build fixtures verify KQ2103 for DMG/dual targets and KQ2102 for constant banks 0 and 8. Runtime verification uses KOKURA; real hardware and interrupt-driven concurrent bank changes are not tested.

__vram_memcpy_unsafe

Copy len bytes from src to dst immediately, in increasing address order. The function returns after the copy finishes; it does not enqueue work. Source and destination advance by one byte, without tilemap row strides or tile-format conversion.

Syntax

c
void __vram_memcpy_unsafe(u16 dst, const void* src, u16 len);

The parameter names in this call syntax explain the compiler intrinsic; they are not a separate C prototype.

Parameters

dst

First destination byte address, normally in VRAM 0x8000–0x9FFF. Keep the entire span valid. CGB uses the current VRAM bank; bank 0 contains tile numbers and bank 1 contains map attributes. These routines do not choose a map from LCDC, wrap rows or allocate VRAM.

src

Source byte pointer, valid and mapped for the whole call. Data is read immediately; it is not retained for a later flush. The source bank is not switched automatically. Use non-overlapping spans: this forward copy does not provide memmove semantics.

len

Number of consecutive bytes, 0–65535. It is not a tile count. Keep the complete address range inside valid source and writable destination storage; the API does not check bounds or prevent 16-bit address wrap.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Skip all LCD/STAT waiting and write directly with the CPU. Keep the LCD off, or ensure the entire transfer fits in a writable interval you control. Merely entering VBlank does not make an arbitrarily long transfer safe. This intrinsic emits neither DI nor EI for the transfer and provides no clipping or bounds checks.

Usage example

c
// The LCD is off throughout these unchecked writes.
__vram_memcpy_unsafe(0x98C2,strip,computed_length());

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This example uses __vram_memcpy_unsafe to place three map bytes at tile (2,6), pixel (16,48). Expect the three patterns [1,2,3] from left to right in a 24 × 8 area. Neighboring cells must remain blank. The full program places each tested copy or fill on its own row.

The supplied original graphics use tile 1 for a solid 8 × 8 square, tile 2 for its left four columns, and tile 3 for a left-aligned triangle that widens by one pixel per row from 1 to 8 pixels. DMG uses black on white; CGB distinguishes the operations with colors while keeping text black.

The square at pixel (16,88) must remain after zero-length safe and unsafe calls. With the LCD on, safe copy and fill add squares at (16,96) and (32,96). FAILED CHECKS must read 000. The emulator tests compare the drawn pixels as well as the counter. CGB uses bank 0 in this example; other banks and physical hardware are not tested here.

In CGB mode, the operation rows from MEMCPY through FILL UNSAFE cycle through red, blue and green. The ZERO COUNT square is red. In LCD ON, the left square is blue and the right square is green. Text stays black. The checks verify these colors at every drawn pixel.

value

len

__far_memcpy

Temporarily select a ROM bank, copy len bytes from src to dst in increasing address order, then restore the compiler’s saved ROM bank. __farmemcpy and __far_memcpy use the same implementation on this platform.

Syntax

c
void __far_memcpy(void* dst, u8 bank, const void* src, u16 len);

Parameters

dst

Destination in writable memory. Reserve enough space for every byte copied. Except for a zero-byte transfer, a null or invalid destination is not checked.

bank

8-bit bank selector for the source ROM mapping. Use a bank supported by the cartridge and the compiler’s bank layout; this argument is separate from the source address.

src

Near pointer to readable source bytes in the selected ROM bank. It does not contain a bank number.

len

Byte count, 0–65535. Zero performs no memory copy, but the bank-selection and restoration steps still run.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Keep the full source range in the intended ROM mapping and the full destination range in writable memory. Copying does not select the next bank at a window boundary, handle overlapping ranges like memmove, or provide VRAM-safe writes. Use the graphics transfer APIs for VRAM.

The samples place calling code in fixed bank 0 so source-bank changes cannot unmap the code being executed. These transfers are synchronous. Do not interrupt a transfer with another bank-changing copy or assume the helper makes a whole application interrupt-safe.

The GB helper restores the compiler’s ROM-bank shadow. Use __bankswitch to maintain it; raw writes to the mapper do not update that shadow. Its saved-bank slot in HRAM is shared, so nested transfers that overwrite it are unsafe.

Usage example

c
__far_memcpy(buffer,2,asset_shapes,32); // Same backend as __farmemcpy.
expect(*asset_bank_marker==0xA1,29);
for(i=0;i<32;i++) expect(buffer[(__safe_index u8)i]==expected_shape_byte(i),32);
buffer[32]=0x55;
__far_memcpy(buffer+32,2,asset_shapes,0); // Zero count writes no bytes and restores the bank.
expect(buffer[32]==0x55 && *asset_bank_marker==0xA1,30);

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

Copy two patterns, 32 bytes, from payload bank 2 while bank 1 is initially selected. The program compares every copied byte, checks that bank 1 is restored and verifies that a zero-length copy leaves a 0x55 sentinel unchanged.

Expected image: a red row starts at pixel (16,32) and spans 72×8 pixels. It repeats an 8×8 triangle, an 8×8 square outline with a one-pixel border, and a tile with its left four columns filled, three times. The triangle grows from one pixel at the top left to eight pixels on its bottom row.

On GB, a blue triangle and outline at (16,64) show the __farmemcpy result; a green pair at (16,96) shows __far_memcpy. Each pair occupies 16×8 pixels. CGB shows these colors; DMG shows the same shapes in black on white.

Expected status: FAILED CHECKS 000 and FIRST FAILURE 00. The verification runs 300 frames and compares text, shape masks and colors against these expectations. This is emulator evidence, not a physical-hardware test.

Complete example program

Build command

powershell
