kitaqgb/lib/rpg.h:148

__fill_tilemap

__fill_tilemap is a compiler alias for __vram_memset. It uses the same byte-count argument and the same immediate CPU transfer, including its LCD-access rules.

Immediately fill len consecutive bytes starting at dst with the byte value. This repeats one byte, not a whole tile pattern. A map-byte fill repeats a tile number; a pattern-byte fill instead changes the encoded pixel planes.

Syntax

c
void __fill_tilemap(u16 dst, u8 value, u16 len);

Parameters

dst

First destination byte address, normally in VRAM 0x8000–0x9FFF. Keep the entire span valid. CGB uses the current VRAM bank; bank 0 contains tile numbers and bank 1 contains map attributes. These routines do not choose a map from LCDC, wrap rows or allocate VRAM.

value

One byte, 0–255, written repeatedly. Its visible meaning depends on whether the destination contains tile indices, pixel planes or another byte-based structure.

len

Number of consecutive bytes, 0–65535. It is not a tile count. Keep the complete address range inside valid source and writable destination storage; the API does not check bounds or prevent 16-bit address wrap.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Supply an explicit starting address and byte count. This alias neither chooses the background map nor fills a whole map automatically. For one complete 32 × 32 tile-number map, use its base address and 1024 bytes with VRAM bank 0 selected.

With the LCD on, each byte write waits for STAT mode 0 or 1 under DI and is followed by EI. With the LCD off, a plain CPU loop is used. The call can span frames, does not preserve an interrupt-disabled entry state, and does not program DMA registers. Zero length performs no data transfer.

Usage example

c
__fill_tilemap(0x9922,1,length_base+length_extra); // Explicit address and byte count, not a whole-map clear.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This example uses __fill_tilemap to fill three map bytes at tile (2,9), pixel (16,72), with tile number 1. Expect a solid 24 × 8 bar with blank neighboring cells. The count is computed from variables or a function result; all three bytes must still receive value 1.

The supplied original graphics use tile 1 for a solid 8 × 8 square, tile 2 for its left four columns, and tile 3 for a left-aligned triangle that widens by one pixel per row from 1 to 8 pixels. DMG uses black on white; CGB distinguishes the operations with colors while keeping text black.

The square at pixel (16,88) must remain after zero-length safe and unsafe calls. With the LCD on, safe copy and fill add squares at (16,96) and (32,96). FAILED CHECKS must read 000. The emulator tests compare the drawn pixels as well as the counter. CGB uses bank 0 in this example; other banks and physical hardware are not tested here.

In CGB mode, the operation rows from MEMCPY through FILL UNSAFE cycle through red, blue and green. The ZERO COUNT square is red. In LCD ON, the left square is blue and the right square is green. Text stays black. The checks verify these colors at every drawn pixel.

__settile_col

Copy len successive tile numbers from src vertically, starting at (x,y) in the LCDC-selected BG map. The source advances one byte and the destination 32 bytes per tile.

Waits for writable VRAM when necessary. The LCD-on path uses interrupt masking and can enable interrupts afterward; it is not a promise to preserve the caller's interrupt state or to finish within one VBlank.

Syntax

c
void __settile_col(u8 x, u8 y, const u8* src, u8 len);

Parameters

x, y

Coordinates in 8×8 tiles, each from 0 to 31. Keep the complete write inside the 32×32 map; coordinates are not clipped.

src

Packed source bytes in RAM or a visible ROM bank. A tile stream contains tile numbers; an attribute stream contains CGB attribute bytes. The routine copies now and does not retain the pointer.

len

Number of bytes, 0–255. Zero copies nothing. Supply at least this many readable source bytes.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Tile bytes go to the currently selected VRAM bank. On CGB, select bank 0 for tile numbers; this call does not switch banks for you.

For a horizontal copy keep x+len <= 32; for a vertical copy keep y+len <= 32. The routine does not wrap coordinates at the map edge.

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

Usage example

c
// __settile_col
// Copy `len` successive tile numbers from `src` vertically, starting at `(x,y)` in the
// LCDC-selected BG map. The source advances one byte and the destination 32 bytes per
// tile. Waits for writable VRAM when necessary. The LCD-on path uses interrupt masking
// and can enable interrupts afterward; it is not a promise to preserve the caller's
// interrupt state or to finish within one VBlank.
// Expected: FAILED CHECKS 000, with the written tile data in the selected map.
#include "gb_tile_color_example.h"
const u8 example_tiles[] = {'A','B','C'};

void main() {
    u8 failures;
    failures = 0;
    tile_color_example_begin();
    __settile_col(2, 3, example_tiles, 3);
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C62, 0) != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9CA2, 0) != 'C') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C63, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    tile_example_finish(failures, 1);
}

Expected result

The selected map shows the written characters or filled area. The program reads back the addressed tile bytes and, on CGB, the attribute bytes; FAILED CHECKS 000 means those comparisons passed. Both maps initially contain zero, and the example selects 0x9C00 for BG/window before the demonstrated call, so a fixed-map call can be distinguished from a selected-map call. These examples perform the writes with the LCD off; they do not measure LCD-on timing.

On CGB, the sample characters and status text are blue on white. The setup gives palette 0 a blue ink color; it does not change the tile-map attributes. On DMG, the same characters are black on white.

Expected image: the following tile pattern starts at tile coordinates (x=2, y=3), or pixel coordinates (16,24). Each character occupies one 8×8 tile. Read the pattern from top to bottom and left to right; the surrounding tiles remain blank.

text
A
B
C

__settile_rect

Fill a w by h rectangle in the LCDC-selected BG map with one tile number. Rows are 32 bytes apart; this repeats a tile, rather than copying an image array.

Waits for writable VRAM when necessary. The LCD-on path uses interrupt masking and can enable interrupts afterward; it is not a promise to preserve the caller's interrupt state or to finish within one VBlank.

Syntax

c
void __settile_rect(u8 x, u8 y, u8 w, u8 h, u8 tile);

Parameters

x, y

Coordinates in 8×8 tiles, each from 0 to 31. Keep the complete write inside the 32×32 map; coordinates are not clipped.

w, h

Width and height in 8×8 tiles. Keep x+w <= 32 and y+h <= 32. A zero dimension performs no write.

tile

Tile number, 0–255. LCDC bit 4 determines how the number addresses tile-image data. It is not a VRAM byte address.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Tile bytes go to the currently selected VRAM bank. On CGB, select bank 0 for tile numbers; this call does not switch banks for you.

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

__settile_xy

__settilemap_rect

Copy a tightly packed w*h array of tile numbers to a rectangle at (x,y) in the map selected by base. Source rows have w bytes; destination rows have a 32-byte stride.

Waits for writable VRAM when necessary. The LCD-on path uses interrupt masking and can enable interrupts afterward; it is not a promise to preserve the caller's interrupt state or to finish within one VBlank.

Syntax

c
void __settilemap_rect(u16 base, u8 x, u8 y, u8 w, u8 h, const u8* src);

Parameters

base

base explicitly selects the map (0x9800 or 0x9C00); the byte address is base + 32*y + x.

x, y

Coordinates in 8×8 tiles, each from 0 to 31. Keep the complete write inside the 32×32 map; coordinates are not clipped.

w, h

Width and height in 8×8 tiles. Keep x+w <= 32 and y+h <= 32. A zero dimension performs no write.

src

Packed source bytes in RAM or a visible ROM bank. A tile stream contains tile numbers; an attribute stream contains CGB attribute bytes. The routine copies now and does not retain the pointer.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

Tile bytes go to the currently selected VRAM bank. On CGB, select bank 0 for tile numbers; this call does not switch banks for you.

The example uses gb_common.h: m_init() loads the supplied ASCII font with character codes as tile numbers. m_wait(); M_LCDC=0; stops the LCD during VBlank before unsafe transfers. The final LCDC value selects which map is visible.

Usage example

c
// __settilemap_rect
// Copy a tightly packed `w*h` array of tile numbers to a rectangle at `(x,y)` in the map
// selected by `base`. Source rows have `w` bytes; destination rows have a 32-byte
// stride. Waits for writable VRAM when necessary. The LCD-on path uses interrupt masking
// and can enable interrupts afterward; it is not a promise to preserve the caller's
// interrupt state or to finish within one VBlank.
// Expected: FAILED CHECKS 000, with the written tile data in the selected map.
#include "gb_tile_color_example.h"
const u8 example_tiles[] = {'A','B','C','D','E','F'};

void main() {
    u8 failures;
    failures = 0;
    tile_color_example_begin();
    __settilemap_rect(0x9C00, 2, 3, 3, 2, example_tiles);
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C62, 0) != 'A') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C84, 0) != 'F') { failures++; }
    if (tile_example_read(0x9C65, 0) != 0) { failures++; }
    tile_example_finish(failures, 1);
}

Expected result

The selected map shows the written characters or filled area. The program reads back the addressed tile bytes and, on CGB, the attribute bytes; FAILED CHECKS 000 means those comparisons passed. Both maps initially contain zero, and the example selects 0x9C00 for BG/window before the demonstrated call, so a fixed-map call can be distinguished from a selected-map call. These examples perform the writes with the LCD off; they do not measure LCD-on timing.

On CGB, the sample characters and status text are blue on white. The setup gives palette 0 a blue ink color; it does not change the tile-map attributes. On DMG, the same characters are black on white.

Expected image: the following tile pattern starts at tile coordinates (x=2, y=3), or pixel coordinates (16,24). Each character occupies one 8×8 tile. Read the pattern from top to bottom and left to right; the surrounding tiles remain blank.

text
ABC
DEF

Complete example program

Build command

powershell
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force .\out | Out-Null
.\kitaqgb\kitaqgb.exe .\kitaq-docs\samples\api-examples\gb\__settilemap_rect.c -I .\kitaqgb\lib -I .\kitaq-docs\samples -o .\out\__settilemap_rect.gb --profile=dev --rst-disable --stack-bank=fixed --cgb=cgb --no-disasm

__vram_copy_dma

__vram_copy_dma is a compiler alias for __vram_memcpy. It uses the same byte-count argument and the same immediate CPU transfer, including its LCD-access rules.

Copy len bytes from src to dst immediately, in increasing address order. The function returns after the copy finishes; it does not enqueue work. Source and destination advance by one byte, without tilemap row strides or tile-format conversion.

Syntax

c
void __vram_copy_dma(u16 dst, const void* src, u16 len);

Parameters

dst

First destination byte address, normally in VRAM 0x8000–0x9FFF. Keep the entire span valid. CGB uses the current VRAM bank; bank 0 contains tile numbers and bank 1 contains map attributes. These routines do not choose a map from LCDC, wrap rows or allocate VRAM.

src

Source byte pointer, valid and mapped for the whole call. Data is read immediately; it is not retained for a later flush. The source bank is not switched automatically. Use non-overlapping spans: this forward copy does not provide memmove semantics.

len

Number of consecutive bytes, 0–65535. It is not a tile count. Keep the complete address range inside valid source and writable destination storage; the API does not check bounds or prevent 16-bit address wrap.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

The name does not select a DMA engine or schedule work for a later HBlank. The caller waits for completion. With the LCD active, the same CPU polling accepts either mode 0 (HBlank) or mode 1 (VBlank); CGB HDMA is not configured.

With the LCD on, each byte write waits for STAT mode 0 or 1 under DI and is followed by EI. With the LCD off, a plain CPU loop is used. The call can span frames, does not preserve an interrupt-disabled entry state, and does not program DMA registers. Zero length performs no data transfer.

Usage example

c
__vram_copy_dma(0x98A2,strip,3); // This alias also uses CPU writes, not a DMA engine.

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This example uses __vram_copy_dma to place three map bytes at tile (2,5), pixel (16,40). Expect the three patterns [1,2,3] from left to right in a 24 × 8 area. Neighboring cells must remain blank. The full program places each tested copy or fill on its own row.

The supplied original graphics use tile 1 for a solid 8 × 8 square, tile 2 for its left four columns, and tile 3 for a left-aligned triangle that widens by one pixel per row from 1 to 8 pixels. DMG uses black on white; CGB distinguishes the operations with colors while keeping text black.

The square at pixel (16,88) must remain after zero-length safe and unsafe calls. With the LCD on, safe copy and fill add squares at (16,96) and (32,96). FAILED CHECKS must read 000. The emulator tests compare the drawn pixels as well as the counter. CGB uses bank 0 in this example; other banks and physical hardware are not tested here.

In CGB mode, the operation rows from MEMCPY through FILL UNSAFE cycle through red, blue and green. The ZERO COUNT square is red. In LCD ON, the left square is blue and the right square is green. Text stays black. The checks verify these colors at every drawn pixel.

Complete example program

Build command

powershell
New-Item -ItemType Directory -Force .\out | Out-Null
.\kitaqgb\kitaqgb.exe .\kitaq-docs\samples\api-examples\gb\vram_memory_shapes.c -I .\kitaqgb\lib -I .\kitaq-docs\samples -o .\out\vram_memory_shapes.gb --profile=dev --rst-disable --stack-bank=fixed --cgb=cgb --no-cache --no-disasm

__vram_fill

__vram_fill is a compiler alias for __vram_memset. It uses the same byte-count argument and the same immediate CPU transfer, including its LCD-access rules.

Immediately fill len consecutive bytes starting at dst with the byte value. This repeats one byte, not a whole tile pattern. A map-byte fill repeats a tile number; a pattern-byte fill instead changes the encoded pixel planes.

Syntax

c
void __vram_fill(u16 dst, u8 value, u16 len);

Parameters

dst

First destination byte address, normally in VRAM 0x8000–0x9FFF. Keep the entire span valid. CGB uses the current VRAM bank; bank 0 contains tile numbers and bank 1 contains map attributes. These routines do not choose a map from LCDC, wrap rows or allocate VRAM.

value

One byte, 0–255, written repeatedly. Its visible meaning depends on whether the destination contains tile indices, pixel planes or another byte-based structure.

len

Number of consecutive bytes, 0–65535. It is not a tile count. Keep the complete address range inside valid source and writable destination storage; the API does not check bounds or prevent 16-bit address wrap.

Return value

None (void).

Conditions and limits

With the LCD on, each byte write waits for STAT mode 0 or 1 under DI and is followed by EI. With the LCD off, a plain CPU loop is used. The call can span frames, does not preserve an interrupt-disabled entry state, and does not program DMA registers. Zero length performs no data transfer.

Usage example

c
__vram_fill(0x9902,1,computed_length());

This is a calling fragment. Follow the stated initialization and buffer requirements before using it in a complete program.

Expected result

This example uses __vram_fill to fill three map bytes at tile (2,8), pixel (16,64), with tile number 1. Expect a solid 24 × 8 bar with blank neighboring cells. The count is computed from variables or a function result; all three bytes must still receive value 1.

The supplied original graphics use tile 1 for a solid 8 × 8 square, tile 2 for its left four columns, and tile 3 for a left-aligned triangle that widens by one pixel per row from 1 to 8 pixels. DMG uses black on white; CGB distinguishes the operations with colors while keeping text black.

The square at pixel (16,88) must remain after zero-length safe and unsafe calls. With the LCD on, safe copy and fill add squares at (16,96) and (32,96). FAILED CHECKS must read 000. The emulator tests compare the drawn pixels as well as the counter. CGB uses bank 0 in this example; other banks and physical hardware are not tested here.

In CGB mode, the operation rows from MEMCPY through FILL UNSAFE cycle through red, blue and green. The ZERO COUNT square is red. In LCD ON, the left square is blue and the right square is green. Text stays black. The checks verify these colors at every drawn pixel.

__wait_vblankCompiler intrinsic

Wait for the next display update. Avoid accidentally waiting twice in one game frame.

c
void __wait_vblank();

        waited++;
        Link_Poll();
        if (Link_TryReadByte(out) != 0) return 1;
    }

Command syntax reference

The following help was captured for the source edition. Consult it for optional arguments, defaults and accepted values. Replace the illustrative input filenames with your own outputs. Captured tool responses retain their original wording.

kitaqgb
help
usage: kitaqgb first.c second.c ... [-o out.gb] [-O0|-O1] [-I dir|--include-dir=dir] [--profile=dev|release|test] [--strict|--permissive] [--rom-header=header.json] [--rom-title=TITLE] [--header-logo=on|off|--no-header-logo] [--cgb=dmg|cgb|cgb_only] [--cart=romonly|mbc1|mbc3|mbc5|...] [--romsize=32k|64k|128k|256k|512k|1m|2m|4m|8m] [--ramsize=none|2k|8k|32k|64k|128k] [--sgb=on|off] [--dest=jp|nonjp] [--version=N] [--trace[=tokens,ast,ir,asm] [--trace-out=<dir>]] [--debug-output | --debug-out[=<dir>]] [--no-disasm|--fast-build] [--disasm-changed[=<git_base>] [--disasm-changed-out=<file>]] [--cache|--no-cache] [--diag-json[=<file>]] [--emit-ai-metadata=<file>] [--emit-path-manifest=<file>] [--machine-readable] [--no-banner] [--stack-bank=fixed|wramx1] [--stack-top=ADDR] [--stack-reserve=N] [--deps-out[=<file>]] [vlist|--vlist[=<file>]] [--max-errors=N] [--abi=legacy|stack] [--bank-sim[=<file>]] [--farcall-suggest[=<file>]] [--cross-bank-report[=<file>]] [--abi-verify[=<file>]] [--abi-diff-report[=<file>]] [--rst-report[=<file>]] [--opt-diff[=<file>]] [--func-size-report[=<file>]] [--hotspot-report[=<file>]] [--repro-check[=<file>]] [--repro-pack[=<dir>]] [--cgb-consistency[=<file>]] [--verify-cgb-symbols[=<file>]] [--minimize[=<file.c>] [--minimize-out=<out.c>] [--minimize-work=<dir>] [--minimize-quick] [--minimize-trace=final|all] [--minimize-on-fail]] [--rst-enable|--rst-on] [--rst-disable|--no-rst] [--rst-use-38] [--rst-speed-safe] [--rst-safe|--rst-unsafe] [--rst-max-calls=N] [--rst-exclude=a,b] [--rst-max-vectors=N] [--watch]
subcommands: kitaqgb test | attrviz | src2asm | symfind | romdiff | kqhelp | template | fixhint | irsum | conventions | snippet | devserver | recipe
  --stack-bank: select WRAM window used for initial CPU stack
  --stack-top: initial SP value inside selected stack window
  --stack-reserve: reserve N bytes below stack top from automatic placement

Developer command syntax

These usages come from Program.DebugTools.cs and Program.VibeTools.cs. ROM comparison, symbol search and templates are subcommands separate from ordinary C compilation.

usage
usage: kitaqgb kqhelp <KQxxxx>
usage
usage: kitaqgb symfind <pattern> [--map=<.map>] [--dbg=<.dbg>] [--regex] [--ignore-case] [--out=<file>|-]
usage
usage: kitaqgb src2asm <source.c:line> [--disasm=<dis.s>] [--funcsizes=<.funcsizes.txt>] [--context=N] [--out=<file>|-]
usage
usage: kitaqgb romdiff <old.gb> <new.gb> [--old-func=<.funcsizes.txt>] [--new-func=<.funcsizes.txt>] [--old-map=<.map>] [--new-map=<.map>] [--out=<file>|-]
usage
usage: kitaqgb template [out.c] [--out=<file.c>] [--cgb] [--overwrite] [--no-build-script] [--no-readme]
usage
usage: kitaqgb conventions [--out=<file>|--stdout]
usage
usage: kitaqgb snippet list | get <id> [--out=<file>|-] | emit <category|all> [--out=<file>|-]
usage
usage: kitaqgb fixhint [diag.json|stderr.log] [--in=<file>] [--out=<file>|-] [--max=N]
usage
usage: kitaqgb irsum [--ir=<syntax_tree_lowered.txt>] [--asm=<assembly_code.txt>] [--top=N] [--out=<file>|-]
usage
usage: kitaqgb devserver [--poll-ms=N] [--debounce-ms=N] [--once] [--max-builds=N] [--deps=<file>] <compile args...>
usage
usage: kitaqgb recipe [--history=<file>] [--last=N] [--out=<file>] [--script=<file>] [--stdout]
